<span class="atom-split" data-atom="the_narrative_of_arthur_gordon_pym_of_nantucket" data-title="The Narrative of Arthur Gordon Pym of Nantucket" data-chapter="" data-kind="intro"></span>


THE NARRATIVE OF [[characters/arthur|ARTHUR]] GORDON PYM.



OF NANTUCKET.





COMPRISING THE DETAILS OF A MUTINY AND ATROCIOUS BUTCHERY ON BOARD THE

AMERICAN BRIG GRAMPUS, ON HER WAY TO THE SOUTH SEAS, IN THE MONTH OF

JUNE, 1827.



WITH AN ACCOUNT OF THE RECAPTURE OF THE VESSEL BY THE SURVIVERS; THEIR

SHIPWRECK AND SUBSEQUENT HORRIBLE SUFFERINGS FROM FAMINE; THEIR

DELIVERANCE BY MEANS OF THE BRITISH SCHOONER JANE GUY; THE BRIEF CRUISE

OF THIS LATTER VESSEL IN THE ANTARCTIC OCEAN; HER CAPTURE, AND THE

MASSACRE OF HER CREW AMONG A GROUP OF ISLANDS IN THE EIGHTY-FOURTH

PARALLEL OF SOUTHERN LATITUDE; TOGETHER WITH THE INCREDIBLE ADVENTURES

AND DISCOVERIES STILL FARTHER SOUTH TO WHICH THAT DISTRESSING CALAMITY

GAVE RISE.









NEW-YORK:

HARPER & BROTHERS, 82 CLIFF-ST.

1838.









Entered, according to Act of Congress, in the year 1838, by



HARPER & BROTHERS,



in the Clerk's Office of the Southern District of New-York.









PREFACE.





Upon my return to the United States a few months ago, after the

extraordinary series of adventure in the South Seas and elsewhere, of

which an account is given in the following pages, accident threw me

into the society of several gentlemen in Richmond, Va., who felt deep

interest in all matters relating to the regions I had visited, and who

were constantly urging it upon me, as a duty, to give my narrative to

the public. I had several reasons, however, for declining to do so,

some of which were of a [[concepts/nature|nature]] altogether private, and concern no

person but myself; others not so much so. One consideration which

deterred me was, that, having kept no journal during a greater portion

of the [[motifs/time|time]] in which I was absent, I feared I should not be able to

write, from mere [[motifs/memory|memory]], a statement so minute and connected as to have

the _appearance_ of that [[concepts/truth|truth]] it would really possess, barring only

the natural and unavoidable exaggeration to which all of us are prone

when detailing events which have had powerful influence in exciting the

imaginative faculties. Another reason was, that the incidents to be

narrated were of a [[concepts/nature|nature]] so positively marvellous, that, unsupported

as my assertions must necessarily be (except by the evidence of a

single individual, and he a half-breed Indian), I could only [[concepts/hope|hope]] for

belief among my family, and those of my friends who have had reason,

through life, to put [[concepts/faith|faith]] in my veracity--the probability being that

the public at large would regard what I should put forth as merely an

impudent and ingenious fiction. A distrust in my own abilities as a

writer was, nevertheless, one of the principal causes which prevented

me from complying with the suggestions of my advisers.



Among those gentlemen in Virginia who expressed the greatest interest

in my statement, more particularly in regard to that portion of it

which related to the Antarctic Ocean, was Mr. Poe, lately editor of the

Southern Literary Messenger, a monthly magazine, published by Mr.

Thomas W. White, in the [[motifs/city|city]] of Richmond. He strongly advised me, among

others, to prepare at once a full account of what I had seen and

undergone, and trust to the shrewdness and common sense of the

public--insisting, with great plausibility, that however roughly, as

regards mere authorship, my book should be got up, its very

uncouthness, if there were any, would give it all the better chance of

being received as [[concepts/truth|truth]].



Notwithstanding this representation, I did not make up my mind to do as

he suggested. He afterward proposed (finding that I would not stir in

the matter) that I should allow him to draw up, in his own words, a

narrative of the earlier portion of my adventures, from facts afforded

by myself, publishing it in the Southern Messenger _under the garb of

fiction_. To this, perceiving no objection, I consented, stipulating

only that my real name should be retained. Two numbers of the pretended

fiction appeared, consequently, in the Messenger for January and

February (1837), and, in order that it might certainly be regarded as

fiction, the name of Mr. Poe was affixed to the articles in the table

of contents of the magazine.



The manner in which this _ruse_ was received has induced me at length

to undertake a regular compilation and publication of the adventures in

question; for I found that, in spite of the air of [[forms/fable|fable]] which had been

so ingeniously thrown around that portion of my statement which

appeared in the Messenger (without altering or distorting a single

fact), the public were still not at all disposed to receive it as

[[forms/fable|fable]], and several letters were sent to Mr. P.'s address distinctly

expressing a conviction to the contrary. I thence concluded that the

facts of my narrative would prove of such a [[concepts/nature|nature]] as to carry with

them sufficient evidence of their own authenticity, and that I had

consequently little to [[concepts/fear|fear]] on the score of popular incredulity.



This _exposé_ being made, it will be seen at once how much of what

follows I claim to be my own writing; and it will also be understood

that no fact is misrepresented in the first few pages which were

written by Mr. Poe. Even to those readers who have not seen the

Messenger, it will be unnecessary to point out where his portion ends

and my own commences; the difference in point of style will be readily

perceived.



A. G. PYM.



New-York, July, 1838.









NARRATIVE OF A. GORDON PYM.





My name is [[characters/arthur|Arthur]] Gordon Pym. My father was a respectable trader in

sea-stores at Nantucket, where I was born. My maternal grandfather was

an attorney in good practice. He was fortunate in everything, and had

speculated very successfully in stocks of the Edgarton New-Bank, as it

was formerly called. By these and other means he had managed to lay by

a tolerable sum of money. He was more attached to myself, I believe,

than to any other person in the world, and I expected to inherit the

most of his property at his [[motifs/death|death]]. He sent me, at six years of age, to

the school of old Mr. Ricketts, a gentleman with only one arm, and of

eccentric manners--he is [[motifs/well|well]] known to almost every person who has

visited New Bedford. I stayed at his school until I was sixteen, when I

left him for Mr. E. Ronald's academy on the [[motifs/hill|hill]]. Here I became

intimate with the son of Mr. Barnard, a sea captain, who generally

sailed in the employ of Lloyd and Vredenburgh--Mr. Barnard is also very

[[motifs/well|well]] known in New Bedford, and has many relations, I am certain, in

Edgarton. His son was named Augustus, and he was nearly two years older

than myself. He had been on a whaling voyage with his father in the

John Donaldson, and was always talking to me of his adventures in the

South Pacific Ocean. I used frequently to go [[motifs/home|home]] with him, and remain

all day, and sometimes all [[motifs/night|night]]. We occupied the same bed, and he

would be sure to keep me awake until almost light, telling me stories

of the natives of the Island of Tinian, and other places he had visited

in his travels. At last I could not help being interested in what he

said, and by degrees I felt the greatest [[motifs/desire|desire]] to go to sea. I owned a

sail-boat called the [[characters/ariel|Ariel]], and worth about seventy-five dollars. She

had a half-deck or cuddy, and was rigged sloop-fashion--I forget her

tonnage, but she would hold ten persons without much crowding. In this

boat we were in the habit of going on some of the maddest freaks in the

world; and, when I now think of them, it appears to me a thousand

wonders that I am alive to-day.



I will relate one of these adventures by way of introduction to a

longer and more momentous narrative. One [[motifs/night|night]] there was a party at Mr.

Barnard's, and both Augustus and myself were not a little intoxicated

towards the close of it. As usual, in such cases, I took part of his

bed in preference to going [[motifs/home|home]]. He went to [[motifs/sleep|sleep]], as I thought, very

quietly (it being near one when the party broke up), and without saying

a word on his favourite topic. It might have been half an hour from the

[[motifs/time|time]] of our getting in bed, and I was just about falling into a doze,

when he suddenly started up, and swore with a terrible oath that he

would not go to [[motifs/sleep|sleep]] for any [[characters/arthur|Arthur]] Pym in Christendom, when there was

so glorious a breeze from the southwest. I never was so astonished in

my life, not knowing what he intended, and thinking that the wines and

liquors he had drunk had set him entirely beside himself. He proceeded

to talk very coolly, however, saying he knew that I supposed him

intoxicated, but that he was never more sober in his life. He was only

tired, he added, of lying in bed on such a fine [[motifs/night|night]] like a dog, and

was determined to get up and dress, and go out on a frolic with the

boat. I can hardly tell what possessed me, but the words were no sooner

out of his mouth than I felt a thrill of the greatest excitement and

pleasure, and thought his mad idea one of the most delightful and most

reasonable things in the world. It was blowing almost a gale, and the

weather was very cold--it being late in October. I sprang out of bed,

nevertheless, in a kind of ecstasy, and told him I was quite as brave

as himself, and quite as tired as he was of lying in bed like a dog,

and quite as ready for any fun or frolic as any Augustus Barnard in

Nantucket.



We lost no [[motifs/time|time]] in getting on our clothes and hurrying down to the

boat. She was lying at the old decayed wharf by the lumber-yard of

Pankey & Co., and almost thumping her sides out against the rough logs.

Augustus got into her and bailed her, for she was nearly half full of

[[motifs/water|water]]. This being done, we hoisted jib and mainsail, kept full, and

started boldly out to sea.



The [[motifs/wind|wind]], as I before said, blew freshly from the southwest. The [[motifs/night|night]]

was very clear and cold. Augustus had taken the helm, and I stationed

myself by the mast, on the deck of the cuddy. We flew along at a great

rate--neither of us having said a word since casting loose from the

wharf. I now asked my companion what course he intended to steer, and

what [[motifs/time|time]] he thought it probable we should get back. He whistled for a

few minutes, and then said crustily, "_I_ am going to sea--_you_ may go

[[motifs/home|home]] if you think proper." Turning my eyes upon him, I perceived at

once that, in spite of his assumed _nonchalance_, he was greatly

agitated. I could see him distinctly by the light of the [[motifs/moon|moon]]--his face

was paler than any marble, and his hand shook so excessively that he

could scarcely retain hold of the tiller. I found that something had

gone wrong, and became seriously alarmed. At this period I knew little

about the management of a boat, and was now depending entirely upon the

nautical skill of my friend. The [[motifs/wind|wind]], too, had suddenly increased, as

we were fast getting out of the lee of the land--still I was ashamed to

betray any trepidation, and for almost half an hour maintained a

resolute [[motifs/silence|silence]]. I could stand it no longer, however, and spoke to

Augustus about the propriety of turning back. As before, it was nearly

a minute before he made answer, or took any notice of my suggestion.

"By-and-by," said he at length--"[[motifs/time|time]] enough--[[motifs/home|home]] by-and-by." I had

expected a similar reply, but there was something in the tone of these

words which filled me with an indescribable feeling of dread. I again

looked at the speaker attentively. His lips were perfectly livid, and

his knees shook so violently together that he seemed scarcely able to

stand. "For God's sake, Augustus," I screamed, now heartily frightened,

"what ails you?--what is the matter?--what _are_ you going to do?"

"Matter!" he stammered, in the greatest apparent surprise, letting go

the tiller at the same moment, and falling forward into the [[characters/bottom|bottom]] of

the boat--"matter!--why, nothing is the--matter--going

[[motifs/home|home]]--d--d--don't you see?" The whole [[concepts/truth|truth]] now flashed upon me. I flew

to him and raised him up. He was drunk--beastly drunk--he could no

longer either stand, speak, or see. His eyes were perfectly glazed; and

as I let him go in the extremity of my [[concepts/despair|despair]], he rolled like a mere

log into the bilge-[[motifs/water|water]] from which I had lifted him. It was evident

that, during the evening, he had drunk far more than I suspected, and

that his conduct in bed had been the result of a highly-concentrated

state of intoxication--a state which, like [[concepts/madness|madness]], frequently enables

the victim to imitate the outward demeanour of one in perfect

possession of his senses. The coolness of the [[motifs/night|night]] air, however, had

had its usual effect--the mental energy began to yield before its

influence--and the confused perception which he no [[concepts/doubt|doubt]] then had of

his perilous situation had assisted in hastening the catastrophe. He

was now thoroughly insensible, and there was no probability that he

would be otherwise for many hours.



It is hardly possible to conceive the extremity of my terror. The fumes

of the wine lately taken had evaporated, leaving me doubly timid and

irresolute. I knew that I was altogether incapable of managing the

boat, and that a fierce [[motifs/wind|wind]] and strong ebb tide were hurrying us to

destruction. A [[motifs/storm|storm]] was evidently gathering behind us; we had neither

compass nor provisions; and it was clear that, if we held our present

course, we should be out of sight of land before daybreak. These

thoughts, with a crowd of others equally fearful, flashed through my

mind with a bewildering rapidity, and for some moments paralyzed me

beyond the possibility of making any exertion. The boat was going

through the [[motifs/water|water]] at a terrible rate--full before the [[motifs/wind|wind]]--no reef in

either jib or mainsail--running her bows completely under the foam. It

was a thousand wonders she did not broach to--Augustus having let go

the tiller, as I said before, and I being too much agitated to think of

taking it myself. By good luck, however, she kept steady, and gradually

I recovered some degree of presence of mind. Still the [[motifs/wind|wind]] was

increasing fearfully; and whenever we [[motifs/rose|rose]] from a plunge forward, the

sea behind fell combing over our counter, and deluged us with [[motifs/water|water]]. I

was so utterly benumbed, too, in every limb, as to be nearly

unconscious of sensation. At length I summoned up the resolution of

[[concepts/despair|despair]], and rushing to the mainsail, let it go by the run. As might

have been expected, it flew over the bows, and, getting drenched with

[[motifs/water|water]], carried away the mast short off by the board. This latter

accident alone saved me from instant destruction. Under the jib only, I

now boomed along before the [[motifs/wind|wind]], shipping heavy seas occasionally over

the counter, but relieved from the terror of immediate [[motifs/death|death]]. I took

the helm, and breathed with greater [[concepts/freedom|freedom]] as I found that there yet

remained to us a chance of ultimate escape. Augustus still lay

senseless in the [[characters/bottom|bottom]] of the boat; and as there was imminent danger

of his drowning (the [[motifs/water|water]] being nearly a foot deep just where he

fell), I contrived to raise him partially up, and keep him in a sitting

position, by passing a rope round his waist, and lashing it to a

ringbolt in the deck of the cuddy. Having thus arranged everything as

[[motifs/well|well]] as I could in my chilled and agitated condition, I recommended

myself to God, and made up my mind to bear whatever might happen with

all the fortitude in my power.



Hardly had I come to this resolution, when, suddenly, a loud and long

scream or yell, as if from the throats of a thousand demons, seemed to

pervade the whole atmosphere around and above the boat. Never while I

live shall I forget the intense agony of terror I experienced at that

moment. My hair stood erect on my head--I felt the [[motifs/blood|blood]] congealing in

my veins--my heart ceased utterly to beat, and without having once

raised my eyes to learn the source of my alarm, I tumbled headlong and

insensible upon the [[concepts/body|body]] of my fallen companion.



I found myself, upon reviving, in the cabin of a large whaling-[[motifs/ship|ship]]

(the Penguin) bound to Nantucket. Several persons were standing over

me, and Augustus, paler than [[motifs/death|death]], was busily occupied in chafing my

hands. Upon seeing me open my eyes, his exclamations of gratitude and

joy excited alternate laughter and [[motifs/tears|tears]] from the rough-looking

personages who were present. The mystery of our being in existence was

now soon explained. We had been run down by the whaling-[[motifs/ship|ship]], which was

close hauled, beating up to Nantucket with every sail she could venture

to set, and consequently running almost at right angles to our own

course. Several men were on the look-out forward, but did not perceive

our boat until it was an impossibility to avoid coming in

contact--their shouts of warning upon seeing us were what so terribly

alarmed me. The huge [[motifs/ship|ship]], I was told, rode immediately over us with as

much ease as our own little vessel would have passed over a feather,

and without the least perceptible impediment to her progress. Not a

scream arose from the deck of the victim--there was a slight grating

sound to be heard mingling with the roar of [[motifs/wind|wind]] and [[motifs/water|water]], as the

frail bark which was swallowed up rubbed for a moment along the keel of

her destroyer--but this was all. Thinking our boat (which it will be

remembered was dismasted) some mere shell cut adrift as useless, the

captain (Captain E. T. V. Block of New [[settings/london|London]]) was for proceeding on

his course without troubling himself further about the matter. Luckily,

there were two of the look-out who swore positively to having seen some

person at our helm, and represented the possibility of yet saving him.

A discussion ensued, when Block grew angry, and, after a while, said

that "it was no business of his to be eternally watching for

egg-shells; that the [[motifs/ship|ship]] should _not_ put about for any such nonsense;

and if there was a man run down, it was nobody's fault but his own--he

might drown and be d----d," or some language to that effect. Henderson,

the first mate, now took the matter up, being justly indignant, as [[motifs/well|well]]

as the whole [[motifs/ship|ship]]'s crew, at a speech evincing so base a degree of

heartless atrocity. He spoke plainly, seeing himself upheld by the men,

told the captain he considered him a fit subject for the gallows, and

that he would disobey his orders if he were hanged for it the moment he

set his foot on shore. He strode aft, jostling Block (who turned very

pale and made no answer) on one side, and seizing the helm, gave the

word, in a firm voice, _Hard-a-lee!_ The men flew to their posts, and

the [[motifs/ship|ship]] went cleverly about. All this had occupied nearly five

minutes, and it was supposed to be hardly within the bounds of

possibility that any individual could be saved--allowing any to have

been on board the boat. Yet, as the reader has seen, both Augustus and

myself were rescued; and our deliverance seemed to have been brought

about by two of those almost inconceivable pieces of good fortune which

are attributed by the wise and pious to the special interference of

[[concepts/providence|Providence]].



While the [[motifs/ship|ship]] was yet in stays, the mate lowered the jolly-boat and

jumped into her with the very two men, I believe, who spoke up as

having seen me at the helm. They had just left the lee of the vessel

(the [[motifs/moon|moon]] still shining brightly) when she made a long and heavy roll

to windward, and Henderson, at the same moment, starting up in his

seat, bawled out to his crew to _back water_. He would say nothing

else--repeating his cry impatiently, _back [[motifs/water|water]]! back [[motifs/water|water]]!_ The men

put back as speedily as possible; but by this [[motifs/time|time]] the [[motifs/ship|ship]] had gone

round, and gotten fully under headway, although all hands on board were

making great exertions to take in sail. In despite of the danger of the

attempt, the mate clung to the main-[[motifs/chains|chains]] as soon as they came within

his reach. Another huge lurch now brought the starboard side of the

vessel out of [[motifs/water|water]] nearly as far as her keel, when the cause of his

anxiety was rendered obvious enough. The [[concepts/body|body]] of a man was seen to be

affixed in the most singular manner to the smooth and shining [[characters/bottom|bottom]]

(the Penguin was coppered and copper-fastened), and beating violently

against it with every movement of the hull. After several ineffectual

efforts, made during the lurches of the [[motifs/ship|ship]], and at the imminent risk

of swamping the boat, I was finally disengaged from my perilous

situation and taken on board--for the [[concepts/body|body]] proved to be my own. It

appeared that one of the timber-bolts having started and broken a

passage through the copper, it had arrested my progress as I passed

under the [[motifs/ship|ship]], and fastened me in so extraordinary a manner to her

[[characters/bottom|bottom]]. The head of the bolt had made its way through the collar of the

green baize jacket I had on, and through the back part of my neck,

forcing itself out between two sinews and just below the right ear. I

was immediately put to bed--although life seemed to be totally extinct.

There was no surgeon on board. The captain, however, treated me with

every attention--to make amends, I presume, in the eyes of his crew,

for his atrocious behaviour in the previous portion of the adventure.



In the meantime, Henderson had again put off from the [[motifs/ship|ship]], although

the [[motifs/wind|wind]] was now blowing almost a hurricane. He had not been gone many

minutes when he fell in with some fragments of our boat, and shortly

afterward one of the men with him asserted that he could distinguish a

cry for help at intervals amid the roaring of the tempest. This induced

the hardy seamen to persevere in their search for more than half an

hour, although repeated signals to return were made them by Captain

Block, and although every moment on the [[motifs/water|water]] in so frail a boat was

fraught to them with the most imminent and deadly peril. Indeed, it is

nearly impossible to conceive how the small jolly they were in could

have escaped destruction for a single instant. She was built, however,

for the whaling service, and was fitted, as I have since had reason to

believe, with air-boxes, in the manner of some life-boats used on the

coast of Wales.



After searching in vain for about the period of [[motifs/time|time]] just mentioned, it

was determined to get back to the [[motifs/ship|ship]]. They had scarcely made this

resolve when a feeble cry arose from a dark object which floated

rapidly by. They pursued and soon overtook it. It proved to be the

entire deck of the [[characters/ariel|Ariel]]'s cuddy. Augustus was struggling near it,

apparently in the last agonies. Upon getting hold of him it was found

that he was attached by a rope to the floating timber. This rope, it

will be remembered, I had myself tied round his waist, and made fast to

a ringbolt, for the purpose of keeping him in an upright position, and

my so doing, it appeared, had been ultimately the means of preserving

his life. The [[characters/ariel|Ariel]] was slightly put together, and in going down her

frame naturally went to pieces; the deck of the cuddy, as might be

expected, was lifted, by the force of the [[motifs/water|water]] rushing in, entirely

from the main timbers, and floated (with other fragments, no [[concepts/doubt|doubt]]) to

the surface--Augustus was buoyed up with it, and thus escaped a

terrible [[motifs/death|death]].



It was more than an hour after being taken on board the Penguin before

he could give any account of himself, or be made to comprehend the

[[concepts/nature|nature]] of the accident which had befallen our boat. At length he became

thoroughly aroused, and spoke much of his sensations while in the

[[motifs/water|water]]. Upon his first attaining any degree of consciousness, he found

himself beneath the surface, whirling round and round with

inconceivable rapidity, and with a rope wrapped in three or four folds

tightly about his neck. In an instant afterward he felt himself going

rapidly upward, when, his head striking violently against a hard

substance, he again relapsed into insensibility. Upon once more

reviving he was in fuller possession of his reason--this was still,

however, in the greatest degree clouded and confused. He now knew that

some accident had occurred, and that he was in the [[motifs/water|water]], although his

mouth was above the surface, and he could breathe with some [[concepts/freedom|freedom]].

Possibly, at this period, the deck was drifting rapidly before the

[[motifs/wind|wind]], and drawing him after it, as he floated upon his back. Of course,

as long as he could have retained this position, it would have been

nearly impossible that he should be drowned. Presently a surge threw

him directly athwart the deck; and this post he endeavoured to

maintain, screaming at intervals for help. Just before he was

discovered by Mr. Henderson, he had been obliged to relax his hold

through exhaustion, and, falling into the sea, had given himself up for

lost. During the whole period of his struggles he had not the faintest

recollection of the [[characters/ariel|Ariel]], nor of any matters in connexion with the

source of his disaster. A vague feeling of terror and [[concepts/despair|despair]] had taken

entire possession of his faculties. When he was finally picked up,

every power of his mind had failed him; and, as before said, it was

nearly an hour after getting on board the Penguin before he became

fully aware of his condition. In regard to myself--I was resuscitated

from a state bordering very nearly upon [[motifs/death|death]] (and after every other

means had been tried in vain for three hours and a half) by vigorous

friction with flannels bathed in hot oil--a proceeding suggested by

Augustus. The wound in my neck, although of an ugly appearance, proved

of little real consequence, and I soon recovered from its effects.



The Penguin got into port about nine o'[[motifs/clock|clock]] in the morning, after

encountering one of the severest gales ever experienced off Nantucket.

Both Augustus and myself managed to appear at Mr. Barnard's in [[motifs/time|time]] for

breakfast--which, luckily, was somewhat late, owing to the party over

[[motifs/night|night]]. I suppose all at the table were too much fatigued themselves to

notice our jaded appearance--of course, it would not have borne a very

rigid scrutiny. Schoolboys, however, can accomplish wonders in the way

of [[concepts/deception|deception]], and I verily believe not one of our friends in Nantucket

had the slightest suspicion that the terrible story told by some

sailors in town of their having run down a vessel at sea and drowned

some thirty or forty poor devils, had reference either to the [[characters/ariel|Ariel]], my

companion, or myself. We two have since very frequently talked the

matter over--but never without a shudder. In one of our conversations

Augustus frankly confessed to me, that in his whole life he had at no

[[motifs/time|time]] experienced so excruciating a sense of dismay, as when on board

our little boat he first discovered the extent of his intoxication, and

felt himself sinking beneath its influence.












<span class="atom-split" data-atom="chapter_01--part_01" data-title="The Narrative of Arthur Gordon Pym of Nantucket — Chapter 2 (part 1)" data-chapter="Chapter 01" data-kind="excerpt" data-tags="motif/death,motif/desire,motif/home,motif/memory,motif/night,motif/tears,motif/time,motif/well,concept/deception,concept/despair,concept/imagination,concept/longing,concept/melancholy,concept/suffering,character/ariel"></span>


CHAPTER II.





In no affairs of mere prejudice, pro or con, do we deduce inferences

with entire certainty even from the most simple data. It might be

supposed that a catastrophe such as I have just related would have

effectually cooled my incipient passion for the sea. On the contrary, I

never experienced a more ardent [[concepts/longing|longing]] for the wild adventures

incident to the life of a navigator than within a week after our

miraculous deliverance. This short period proved amply long enough to

erase from my [[motifs/memory|memory]] the shadows, and bring out in vivid light all the

pleasurably exciting points of colour, all the picturesqueness of the

late perilous accident. My conversations with Augustus grew daily more

frequent and more intensely full of interest. He had a manner of

relating his stories of the ocean (more than one half of which I now

suspect to have been sheer fabrications) [[motifs/well|well]] adapted to have weight

with one of my enthusiastic temperament, and somewhat gloomy, although

glowing [[concepts/imagination|imagination]]. It is strange, too, that he most strongly enlisted

my feelings in behalf of the life of a seaman, when he depicted his

more terrible moments of [[concepts/suffering|suffering]] and [[concepts/despair|despair]]. For the bright side of

the painting I had a limited sympathy. My visions were of shipwreck and

famine; of [[motifs/death|death]] or captivity among barbarian hordes; of a lifetime

dragged out in sorrow and [[motifs/tears|tears]], upon some gray and desolate rock, in

an ocean unapproachable and unknown. Such visions or desires--for they

amounted to desires--are common, I have since been assured, to the

whole numerous race of the [[concepts/melancholy|melancholy]] among men--at the [[motifs/time|time]] of which I

speak I regarded them only as prophetic glimpses of a destiny which I

felt myself in a measure bound to fulfil. Augustus thoroughly entered

into my state of mind. It is probable, indeed, that our intimate

communion had resulted in a partial interchange of character.



About eighteen months after the period of the [[characters/ariel|Ariel]]'s disaster, the

firm of Lloyd and Vredenburgh (a house connected in some manner with

the Messieurs Enderby, I believe, of Liverpool) were engaged in

repairing and fitting out the brig Grampus for a whaling voyage. She

was an old hulk, and scarcely seaworthy when all was done to her that

could be done. I hardly know why she was chosen in preference to other

good vessels belonging to the same owners--but so it was. Mr. Barnard

was appointed to command her, and Augustus was going with him. While

the brig was getting ready, he frequently urged upon me the excellency

of the opportunity now offered for indulging my [[motifs/desire|desire]] of travel. He

found me by no means an unwilling listener--yet the matter could not be

so easily arranged. My father made no direct opposition; but my mother

went into hysterics at the bare mention of the design; and, more than

all, my grandfather, from whom I expected much, vowed to cut me off

with a shilling if I should ever broach the subject to him again. These

difficulties, however, so far from abating my [[motifs/desire|desire]], only added fuel

to the flame. I determined to go at all hazards; and, having made known

my intention to Augustus, we set about arranging a plan by which it

might be accomplished. In the meantime I forbore speaking to any of my

relations in regard to the voyage, and, as I busied myself ostensibly

with my usual studies, it was supposed that I had abandoned the design.

I have since frequently examined my conduct on this occasion with

sentiments of displeasure as [[motifs/well|well]] as of surprise. The intense hypocrisy

I made use of for the furtherance of my project--an hypocrisy pervading

every word and action of my life for so long a period of [[motifs/time|time]]--could

only have been rendered tolerable to myself by the wild and burning

expectation with which I looked forward to the fulfilment of my

long-cherished visions of travel.



In pursuance of my scheme of [[concepts/deception|deception]], I was necessarily obliged to

leave much to the management of Augustus, who was employed for the

greater part of every day on board the Grampus, attending to some

arrangements for his father in the cabin and cabin hold. At [[motifs/night|night]],

however, we were sure to have a conference, and talk over our hopes.

After nearly a month passed in this manner, without our hitting upon

any plan we thought likely to succeed, he told me at last that he had

determined upon everything necessary. I had a relation living in New

Bedford, a Mr. Ross, at whose house I was in the habit of spending

occasionally two or three weeks at a [[motifs/time|time]]. The brig was to sail about

the middle of June (June, 1827), and it was agreed that, a day or two

before her putting to sea, my father was to receive a note, as usual,

from Mr. Ross, asking me to come over and spend a fortnight with Robert

and Emmet (his sons). Augustus charged himself with the enditing of

this note and getting it delivered. Having set out, as supposed, for

New Bedford, I was then to report myself to my companion, who would

contrive a hiding-place for me in the Grampus. This hiding-place, he

assured me, would be rendered sufficiently comfortable for a residence

of many days, during which I was not to make my appearance. When the

brig had proceeded so far on her course as to make any turning back a

matter out of question, I should then, he said, be formally installed

in all the comforts of the cabin; and as to his father, he would only

laugh heartily at the joke. Vessels enough would be met with by which a

letter might be sent [[motifs/home|home]] explaining the adventure to my parents.



The middle of June at length arrived, and everything had been matured.

The note was written and delivered, and on a Monday morning I left the

house for the New Bedford packet, as supposed. I went, however,

straight to Augustus, who was waiting for me at the corner of a street.

CAPITOLO II.





In nessun caso basato semplicemente su pregiudizi, né a favore né contro, traiamo conclusioni.

Con assoluta certezza, anche a partire dai dati più semplici… Potrebbe essere così.

Si potrebbe supporre che una catastrofe del genere di quella che ho appena descritto avrebbe…

Effettivamente spense la mia nascente passione per il mare… Al contrario, mi…

Non aveva mai provato un desiderio così ardente per le avventure selvagge.

Un incidente nella vita di un navigatore… avvenuto entro una settimana dal nostro arrivo

Miracolosa liberazione. Questo breve lasso di tempo si rivelò più che sufficiente…

Cancellate dalle mie [[motifs/memory|memorie]] tutte quelle ombre, e fate emergere, nella luce più vivida, tutto ciò che.

Punti di colore piacevolmente emozionanti, tutta la pittoresca bellezza…

Un pericoloso incidente… Le mie conversazioni con Augustus diventavano sempre più frequenti…

Più frequente e sempre più ricco di interesse… Aveva un modo di fare particolare…

Titolo:  
Raccontando le sue storie sull’oceano (di cui più della metà… oggi…)

Si sospetta che si tratti soltanto di invenzioni, ma queste sono state ben adattate per sembrare credibili.

Con uno dei miei temperamenti entusiasti… e al contempo piuttosto cupi, sebbene…

una [[concepts/imagination|immaginazione]] brillante. È anche strano che sia stato proprio lui ad essere il più convinto nell’utilizzarla.

I miei sentimenti riguardo alla vita di un marinaio, quando ne descriveva…

Momenti ancora più terribili di [[concepts/suffering|sofferenza]] e [[concepts/despair|disperazione]]. Ma c’è anche il lato positivo.

“La pittura che suscitava in me solo una limitata empatia… Le mie immagini riguardavano naufragi e…”

Fame; la morte o la prigionia tra le orde barbare; una vita di sofferenze.

Trascinato via nella tristezza e tra le [[motifs/tears|lacrime]], su qualche roccia grigia e desolata.

Un oceano inaccessibile e sconosciuto… Tali visioni o desideri… perché…

“Si trattava semplicemente di desideri… sono piuttosto comuni, mi è stato detto in seguito.”

Un'intera, numerosa razza di persone melanconiche tra gli uomini, in quel momento io.

Titolo:  
Li consideravo soltanto come anticipazioni profetiche di un destino che…

Mi sentivo in qualche modo obbligato a portare a termine quella missione. Augustus vi si impegnò appieno.

Nel mio stato d’animo attuale… È probabile, infatti, che il nostro rapporto intimo…

La comunione aveva portato a un parziale scambio di caratteri tra le persone coinvolte.



Circa diciotto mesi dopo il periodo del disastro di [[characters/ariel|Ariel]], il.

Lo studio legale di Lloyd e Vredenburgh (una azienda collegata in qualche modo con, )

I signori Enderby, credo di Liverpool, erano impegnati…

Riparare e attrezzare la brigata Grampus per una spedizione di caccia alle balene. Lei

Era un vecchio relitto, a malapena idoneo alla navigazione, dopo che le erano state apportate tutte le riparazioni necessarie…

Potrebbe essere stato fatto… Non so proprio perché sia stata scelta invece di altre.

“Vessilli buoni appartenenti agli stessi proprietari… Ed era proprio così. Il signor Barnard.”

Fu nominato per comandarla, e Augusto lo accompagnava. Nel frattempo…

La brigata si stava preparando; lui insisteva spesso sull’eccellenza di tale processo.

Di questa opportunità che mi viene offerta ora per soddisfare il mio desiderio di viaggiare. Lui.

Non mi trovai affatto un ascoltatore riluttante… tuttavia, la questione non poteva essere risolta in quel modo.

Tutto fu sistemato con estrema facilità. Mio padre non ebbe obiezioni dirette; ma mia madre…

Entrò in isteria al solo menzionare quel design; e, più ancora…

“Mio nonno, da cui mi aspettavo molto, giurò di tagliarmi i ponti…”

“Con una sterlina, se mai dovessi riprendere l’argomento con lui…”

Tuttavia, le difficoltà, invece di attenuare il mio [[motifs/desire|desiderio]], non hanno fatto altro che alimentarlo ulteriormente.

Verso la fiamma… Decisi di correre tutti i rischi possibili; e, dopo averlo comunicato a tutti, partii.

Il mio intento verso Augusto… iniziammo a preparare un piano per realizzarlo

Potrebbe essere realizzato. Nel frattempo ho deciso di non parlare con nessuno di loro.

Relazioni riguardanti il viaggio… e, mentre apparentemente mi occupavo di altre cose…

Con i miei soliti studi, si pensava che avessi abbandonato quel progetto.

Da allora ho spesso ripensato al mio comportamento in quell’occasione…

Sentimenti di disappunto, così come di sorpresa. L’intensa ipocrisia.

Un’ipocrisia che permea tutto

Ogni parola e ogni azione della mia vita, per un periodo così lungo di [[motifs/time|tempo]], avrebbero potuto.

Solo diventando selvaggio e appassionato sono riuscito a renderlo tollerabile per me stesso…

Con quell’attesa ansiosa con cui aspettavo che si realizzasse il mio desiderio…

Visioni di viaggio a lungo desiderate.



Al fine di attuare il mio piano di [[concepts/deception|inganno]], ero necessariamente costretto a.

Il compito fu lasciato interamente alla gestione di Augusto, che ne fu incaricato.

La maggior parte della giornata a bordo del Grampus viene trascorsa occupandosi di varie attività…

I preparativi per suo padre nella cabina e nel vano bagagli. Di [[motifs/night|notte]],

Tuttavia, eravamo sicuri che avremmo tenuto una riunione per discutere delle nostre speranze.

Dopo quasi un mese trascorso in questo modo, senza che riuscissimo a trovare…

“Qualsiasi piano ritenessimo potesse avere successo”, mi disse infine, “lui lo aveva già scartato”.

Deciso a fare tutto il necessario… Avevo un conoscente che viveva a New…

Bedford, da Mr. Ross, nella cui casa avevo l’abitudine di trascorrere.

A volte, due o tre settimane di [[motifs/time|tempo]]. La brigata avrebbe dovuto salpare entro circa.

A metà di giugno (giugno 1827), fu concordato che… un giorno o due dopo…

Prima di salpare, mio padre ricevette, come al solito, una lettera.

Da parte del signor Ross: mi chiede di andare da lui e trascorrere quindici giorni con Robert

E Emmett (i suoi figli). Augustus si assunse il compito di concludere…

Questa nota e il suo recapito. Dopo essersi messo in viaggio, come previsto…

New Bedford: Dovevo poi presentarmi al mio compagno…

“Trova un nascondiglio per me sul Grampus… Questo nascondiglio…”

Mi assicurò che l’ambiente sarebbe stato abbastanza confortevole per viverci.

“Di molti giorni, durante i quali non dovevo farmi vedere.”

La nave aveva già percorso una tale distanza lungo la sua rotta che qualsiasi tentativo di tornare indietro sarebbe stato impossibile.

“Una volta che la questione è stata risolta senza dubbi, allora dovrei essere formalmente insediato”, disse.

Nella comodità della cabina… e per quanto riguarda suo padre, lui voleva solo…

Ridete di cuore a quella battuta… Ci sarebbero sicuramente molti modi per farlo.

Potrei inviare una lettera a casa per spiegare loro tutta questa avventura.



Finalmente arrivò la metà di giugno, e tutto era ormai maturo.

La nota fu scritta e consegnata; una mattina di lunedì me ne andai.

La casa era destinata al battello postale di New Bedford, come previsto. Tuttavia, io ci sono andato lo stesso.

Direttamente ad Augusto, che mi aspettava all’angolo di una strada.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="chapter_01--part_02" data-title="The Narrative of Arthur Gordon Pym of Nantucket — Chapter 2 (part 2)" data-chapter="Chapter 01" data-kind="excerpt" data-tags="motif/nature,motif/rose,motif/ship,motif/soul,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,concept/nature,setting/ship,character/edmund"></span>


It had been our original plan that I should keep out of the way until

dark, and then slip on board the brig; but, as there was now a thick

fog in our favour, it was agreed to lose no [[motifs/time|time]] in secreting me.

Augustus led the way to the wharf, and I followed at a little distance,

enveloped in a thick seaman's cloak, which he had brought with him, so

that my person might not be easily recognised. Just as we turned the

second corner, after passing Mr. [[characters/edmund|Edmund]]'s [[motifs/well|well]], who should appear,

standing right in front of me, and looking me full in the face, but old

Mr. Peterson, my grandfather. "Why, bless my [[motifs/soul|soul]], Gordon," said he,

after a long pause, "why, why--_whose_ dirty cloak is that you have

on?" "Sir!" I replied, assuming, as [[motifs/well|well]] as I could, in the exigency of

the moment, an air of offended surprise, and talking in the gruffest of

all imaginable tones--"sir! you are a sum'mat mistaken--my name, in the

first place, bee'nt nothing at all like Goddin, and I'd want you for to

know better, you blackguard, than to call my new obercoat a darty one!"

For my life I could hardly refrain from screaming with laughter at the

odd manner in which the old gentleman received this handsome rebuke. He

started back two or three steps, turned first pale and then excessively

red, threw up his spectacles, then, putting them down, ran full tilt at

me, with his umbrella uplifted. He stopped short, however, in his

career, as if struck with a sudden recollection; and presently, turning

round, hobbled off down the street, shaking all the while with rage,

and muttering between his teeth, "Won't do--new glasses--thought it was

Gordon--d----d good-for-nothing salt [[motifs/water|water]] Long Tom."



After this narrow escape we proceeded with greater caution, and arrived

at our point of destination in safety. There were only one or two of

the hands on board, and these were busy forward, doing something to the

forecastle combings. Captain Barnard, we knew very [[motifs/well|well]], was engaged at

Lloyd and Vredenburgh's, and would remain there until late in the

evening, so we had little to apprehend on his account. Augustus went

first up the vessel's side, and in a short while I followed him,

without being noticed by the men at work. We proceeded at once into the

cabin, and found no person there. It was fitted up in the most

comfortable style--a thing somewhat unusual in a whaling-vessel. There

were four very excellent staterooms, with wide and convenient berths.

There was also a large stove, I took notice, and a remarkably thick and

valuable carpet covering the floor of both the cabin and staterooms.

The ceiling was full seven feet high, and, in short, everything

appeared of a more roomy and agreeable [[concepts/nature|nature]] than I had anticipated.

Augustus, however, would allow me but little [[motifs/time|time]] for observation,

insisting upon the necessity of my concealing myself as soon as

possible. He led the way into his own stateroom, which was on the

starboard side of the brig, and next to the bulkheads. Upon entering,

he closed the door and bolted it. I thought I had never seen a nicer

little room than the one in which I now found myself. It was about ten

feet long, and had only one berth, which, as I said before, was wide

and convenient. In that portion of the closet nearest the bulkheads

there was a space of four feet square, containing a table, a chair, and

a set of hanging shelves full of books, chiefly books of voyages and

travels. There were many other little comforts in the room, among which

I ought not to forget a kind of safe or refrigerator, in which Augustus

pointed out to me a host of delicacies, both in the eating and drinking

department.



He now pressed with his knuckles upon a certain spot of the carpet in

one corner of the space just mentioned, letting me know that a portion

of the flooring, about sixteen inches square, had been neatly cut out

and again adjusted. As he pressed, this portion [[motifs/rose|rose]] up at one end

sufficiently to allow the passage of his finger beneath. In this manner

he raised the mouth of the trap (to which the carpet was still fastened

by tacks), and I found that it led into the after hold. He next lit a

small taper by means of a phosphorus match, and, placing the light in a

dark lantern, descended with it through the opening, bidding me follow.

I did so, and he then pulled the cover upon the hole, by means of a

nail driven into the under side--the carpet, of course, resuming its

original position on the floor of the stateroom, and all traces of the

aperture being concealed.



The taper gave out so feeble a ray, that it was with the greatest

difficulty I could grope my way through the confused mass of lumber

among which I now found myself. By degrees, however, my eyes became

accustomed to the gloom, and I proceeded with less trouble, holding on

to the skirts of my friend's coat. He brought me, at length, after

creeping and winding through innumerable narrow passages, to an

iron-bound box, such as is used sometimes for packing fine earthenware.

It was nearly four feet high, and full six long, but very narrow. Two

large empty oil-casks lay on the top of it, and above these, again, a

vast quantity of straw matting, piled up as high as the floor of the

cabin. In every other direction around was wedged as closely as

possible, even up to the ceiling, a complete chaos of almost every

species of [[motifs/ship|ship]]-furniture, together with a heterogeneous medley of

crates, hampers, barrels, and bales, so that it seemed a matter no less

than miraculous that we had discovered any passage at all to the box. I

afterward found that Augustus had purposely arranged the stowage in

this hold with a view to affording me a thorough concealment, having

had only one assistant in the labour, a man not going out in the brig.



My companion now showed me that one of the ends of the box could be

removed at pleasure. He slipped it aside and displayed the interior, at


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

Il nostro piano iniziale era che io rimanessi fuori dai piedi fino a…

Buio profondo… e poi salire a bordo della brigata; ma poiché ora c’era una fitta oscurità…

Se la nebbia fosse stata a nostro favore, si decise di non perdere nemmeno un [[motifs/time|minuto]] nell’organizzare il mio nascondiglio.

Augustus guidò verso il molo, e io lo seguii a una certa distanza.

Avvolto in un spesso mantello da marinaio che aveva portato con sé, così…

Che la mia persona non possa essere facilmente riconosciuta… Proprio mentre ci giravamo…

All’angolo successivo, dopo aver superato il [[motifs/well|pozzo]] del signor [[characters/edmund|Edmund]], che dovrebbe apparire.

In piedi proprio davanti a me, che mi guarda dritto negli occhi… ma vecchio.

Signor Peterson, mio nonno. “Perché, per l’amore della mia [[motifs/soul|'anima]], Gordon,” disse lui.

Dopo una lunga pausa: “Perché… di chi è quel mantello sporco che indossi?”

“Sì, signore!” risposi, cercando di farlo nel modo migliore possibile, data l’urgenza della situazione.

Il momento… un’aria di offesa e sorpresa, e parole pronunciate con la massima asprezza

“Tutti i toni immaginabili… ‘Signore, si sbaglia: il mio nome è…”

Titolo:  
Prima di tutto, non assomiglia affatto a Goddin… e vorrei che tu fossi mio.

Dovresti saperlo meglio tu, mascalzone, che non definire il mio nuovo cappotto un “darty”!

Per la mia vita, faticavo a trattenere le risate…

Il modo strano con cui il vecchio signore accolse questa severa rimprovera…

Indietreggiò di due o tre passi, impallidendo prima e poi diventando estremamente pallido…

Rosso, gettò via gli occhiali e poi, mettendoli giù, corse dritto verso…

“Me, con il suo ombrello sollevato… Tuttavia, si fermò di colpo.”

Titolo:  
“Carriera… come se fosse colpito da un improvviso ricordo; e subito dopo, girandosi…”

"Zoppicando lungo la strada, tremante di rabbia"

“E mormorando tra i denti: ‘Non va… occhiali nuovi… Pensavo fosse…”

Gordon, un vero buono a nulla che salta nell’acqua. Long Tom.



Dopo questa stretta via d’uscita, procedemmo con maggiore cautela e arrivammo…

A nostro arrivo, al sicuro… C’erano solo uno o due…

Le mani a bordo… e queste erano impegnate davanti, a fare qualcosa…

Le ispezioni nella prua. Capitano Barnard, sapevamo molto bene che era impegnato in.

Lloyd e Vredenburgh… e vi sarebbero rimasti fino a tarda notte.

“Serata… quindi non c’era molto da temere per quanto lo riguardava. Augustus se ne andò…”

“Primo sul lato della nave… e dopo poco lo seguii.”

Senza essere notati dagli uomini al lavoro, entrammo immediatamente…

Cabina… e non c’era nessuno dentro. Era arredata nel modo più…

Stile confortevole… qualcosa di piuttosto insolito su una nave baleniera.

C'erano quattro cabine molto eleganti, dotate di letti ampi e comodi.

C’era anche un grande camino; me ne sono accorto, e… notevolmente spesso.

Un prezioso tappeto che copriva il pavimento sia della cabina che della cabina VIP.

Il soffitto era alto sette piedi; insomma, tutto…

Si rivelò avere una natura più spaziosa e piacevole di quanto mi aspettassi.

Tuttavia, Augusto mi concedeva molto poco [[motifs/time|tempo]] per osservare.

Titolo:  
Insistendo sull’urgenza che io mi nasconda immediatamente…

Possibile. Lo guidò nella sua cabina privata, che si trovava…

Lato destro della brigata, vicino ai parati… Entrando lì…

Chiuse la porta e la bloccò con la serratura. Pensai di non aver mai visto nulla di più bello…

Una stanza ancora più piccola di quella in cui mi trovavo ora… Aveva circa dieci metri quadrati.

Titolo:  
Lungo quanto i piedi di una persona, e con soltanto un letto… che, come ho detto prima, era piuttosto largo.

Pratico e comodo… Nella parte dell’armadio più vicina ai parati divisori.

C’era uno spazio di circa quattro piedi quadrati, all’interno del quale c’erano un tavolo e una sedia.

Un insieme di scaffali appesi pieni di libri, principalmente volumi sui viaggi

Viaggi… C’erano molte altre piccole comodità nella stanza, tra cui…

“Non dovrei dimenticare quel tipo di armadio o frigorifero… nel quale Augustus…”

Mi furono indicate molte prelibatezze, sia per quanto riguarda il cibo che la bevanda.

dipartimento.



Ora premetteva le nocche su un certo punto del tappeto…

Un angolo dello spazio appena menzionato…

Una porzione del pavimento, di circa sedici pollici quadrati, era stata tagliata con cura.

E ancora una volta fu regolato. Mentre premeva, questa parte della [[motifs/rose|rosa]] si sollevava dall'estremità.

Abbastanza da permettere il passaggio di un dito sotto di esso. In questo modo…

Apre la bocca della trappola… (dove il tappeto era ancora fissato)

Attraverso dei ganci, riuscii ad entrare nella stiva posteriore. Poi accese…

Una piccola fiamma prodotta con un fiammifero al fosforo… e posizionando quella luce in un certo modo…

Una lanterna scura; scese attraverso quell’apertura, invitandomi a seguirla.

Lo feci, e poi lui coprì il foro usando…

“Un chiodo piantato sul lato inferiore… Il tappeto, naturalmente, riprende il suo aspetto normale.”

Posizione originale sul pavimento della cabina, e tutte le tracce…

Le aperture erano nascoste.



La candela emetteva un raggio così debole che…

"Difficoltà nel trovare la via attraverso quella massa confusa di legname"""

Tra cui mi trovavo ora. Tuttavia, gradualmente, i miei occhi iniziarono a…

Abituato all’oscurità, proseguii senza troppi problemi, tenendomi saldamente attaccato…

Alle falde del cappotto di mio amico… Alla fine, mi portò con sé.

Che si snoda e serpeggia attraverso innumerevoli passaggi stretti, fino a…

Scatola in ferro, quella che a volte viene utilizzata per imballare porcellane delicate.

Aveva quasi quattro piedi di altezza e sei piedi di lunghezza, ma era molto stretto. Due…

“Su di esso giacevano grandi barili vuoti di olio; e al di sopra di questi, ancora…”

Enorme quantità di stuoie di paglia, ammucchiate fino al livello del pavimento

Cabina… In tutte le altre direzioni, tutto era stipato altrettanto strettamente intorno ad essa.

Un caos totale, che sembra raggiungere persino il soffitto… un disordine totale in quasi ogni aspetto.

Varie tipologie di mobili per navi, insieme a un insieme eterogeneo di.

Cassoni, scatole, barili e balle… sembrava davvero una questione di non minore importanza.

Non è affatto miracoloso che siamo riusciti a trovare un qualche passaggio per raggiungere quella scatola.

In seguito si scoprì che Augusto aveva organizzato intenzionalmente il carico in quel modo.

Un rifugio sicuro per garantirmi un nascondiglio perfetto

Aveva soltanto un assistente durante il parto: un uomo che non usciva mai dalla cabina del brigantino.



Il mio compagno mi mostrò che uno dei lati della scatola poteva essere…

Titolo:  
Rimosso su richiesta… Lo mise da parte e mostrò l’interno…



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="chapter_01--part_03" data-title="The Narrative of Arthur Gordon Pym of Nantucket — Chapter 2 (part 3)" data-chapter="Chapter 01" data-kind="excerpt" data-tags="motif/desire,motif/journey,motif/sleep,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,concept/fear,concept/suffering,character/bottom"></span>


which I was excessively amused. A mattress from one of the cabin berths

covered the whole of its [[characters/bottom|bottom]], and it contained almost every article

of mere comfort which could be crowded into so small a space, allowing

me, at the same [[motifs/time|time]], sufficient room for my accommodation, either in a

sitting position or lying at full length. Among other things, there

were some books, pen, ink, and paper, three blankets, a large jug full

of [[motifs/water|water]], a keg of sea-biscuit, three or four immense Bologna sausages,

an enormous ham, a cold leg of roast mutton, and half a dozen bottles

of cordials and liqueurs. I proceeded immediately to take possession of

my little apartment, and this with feelings of higher satisfaction, I

am sure, than any monarch ever experienced upon entering a new palace.

Augustus now pointed out to me the method of fastening the open end of

the box, and then, holding the taper close to the deck, showed me a

piece of dark whipcord lying along it. This, he said, extended from my

hiding-place throughout all the necessary windings among the lumber, to

a nail which was driven into the deck of the hold, immediately beneath

the trapdoor leading into his stateroom. By means of this cord I should

be enabled readily to trace my way out without his guidance, provided

any unlooked-for accident should render such a step necessary. He now

took his departure, leaving with me the lantern, together with a

copious supply of tapers and phosphorus, and promising to pay me a

visit as often as he could contrive to do so without observation. This

was on the seventeenth of June.



I remained three days and nights (as nearly as I could guess) in my

hiding-place without getting out of it at all, except twice for the

purpose of stretching my limbs by standing erect between two crates

just opposite the opening. During the whole period I saw nothing of

Augustus; but this occasioned me little uneasiness, as I knew the brig

was expected to put to sea every hour, and in the bustle he would not

easily find opportunities of coming down to me. At length I heard the

trap open and shut, and presently he called in a low voice, asking if

all was [[motifs/well|well]], and if there was anything I wanted. "Nothing," I replied;

"I am as comfortable as can be; when will the brig sail?" "She will be

under weigh in less than half an hour," he answered. "I came to let you

know, and for [[concepts/fear|fear]] you should be uneasy at my absence. I shall not have

a chance of coming down again for some [[motifs/time|time]]--perhaps for three or four

days more. All is going on right aboveboard. After I go up and close

the trap, do you creep along by the whipcord to where the nail is

driven in. You will find my watch there--it may be useful to you, as

you have no daylight to keep [[motifs/time|time]] by. I suppose you can't tell how long

you have been buried--only three days--this is the twentieth. I would

bring the watch to your box, but am afraid of being missed." With this

he went up.



In about an hour after he had gone I distinctly felt the brig in

motion, and congratulated myself upon having at length fairly commenced

a voyage. Satisfied with this idea, I determined to make my mind as

easy as possible, and await the course of events until I should be

permitted to exchange the box for the more roomy, although hardly more

comfortable, accommodations of the cabin. My first care was to get the

watch. Leaving the taper burning, I groped along in the dark, following

the cord through windings innumerable, in some of which I discovered

that, after toiling a long distance, I was brought back within a foot

or two of a former position. At length I reached the nail, and,

securing the object of my [[motifs/journey|journey]], returned with it in safety. I now

looked over the books which had been so thoughtfully provided, and

selected the expedition of Lewis and Clarke to the mouth of the

Columbia. With this I amused myself for some [[motifs/time|time]], when, growing

sleepy, I extinguished the light with great care, and soon fell into a

sound slumber.



Upon awaking I felt strangely confused in mind, and some [[motifs/time|time]] elapsed

before I could bring to recollection all the various circumstances of

my situation. By degrees, however, I remembered all. Striking a light,

I looked at the watch; but it was run down, and there were,

consequently, no means of determining how long I had slept. My limbs

were greatly cramped, and I was forced to relieve them by standing

between the crates. Presently, feeling an almost ravenous appetite, I

bethought myself of the cold mutton, some of which I had eaten just

before going to [[motifs/sleep|sleep]], and found excellent. What was my astonishment at

discovering it to be in a state of absolute putrefaction! This

circumstance occasioned me great disquietude; for, connecting it with

the disorder of mind I experienced upon awaking, I began to suppose

that I must have slept for an inordinately long period of [[motifs/time|time]]. The

close atmosphere of the hold might have had something to do with this,

and might, in the end, be productive of the most serious results. My

head ached excessively; I fancied that I drew every breath with

difficulty; and, in short, I was oppressed with a multitude of gloomy

feelings. Still I could not venture to make any disturbance by opening

the trap or otherwise, and, having wound up the watch, contented myself

as [[motifs/well|well]] as possible.



Throughout the whole of the next tedious twenty-four hours no person

came to my relief, and I could not help accusing Augustus of the

grossest inattention. What alarmed me chiefly was, that the [[motifs/water|water]] in my

jug was reduced to about half a pint, and I was [[concepts/suffering|suffering]] much from

thirst, having eaten freely of the Bologna sausages after the loss of

my mutton. I became very uneasy, and could no longer take any interest

in my books. I was overpowered, too, with a [[motifs/desire|desire]] to [[motifs/sleep|sleep]], yet


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

Un materasso proveniente da uno dei letti della cabina… che mi divertì moltissimo.

Copriva l’intero suo [[characters/bottom|basso]] ed era pieno di quasi tutti gli oggetti possibili.

Titolo:  
Un semplice conforto che può trovare spazio in uno spazio così ristretto…

Io, allo stesso [[motifs/time|tempo]], avrei bisogno di abbastanza spazio per il mio soggiorno, sia che si tratti di.

Posizione seduta o distesa completamente… Tra le altre cose, ciò…

Libri, penna, inchiostro e carta… tre coperte, una grande giara piena…

Acqua, una botte di biscotti al mare, tre o quattro enormi salsicce di Bologna.

Un enorme prosciutto, una coscia di montone arrosto fredda e mezza dozzina di bottiglie

Di bevande digestive e liquori… Mi misi immediatamente all’opera per prenderne possesso.

Il mio piccolo appartamento… e questo con sentimenti di grande soddisfazione.

Ne sono sicuro: un’emozione più intensa di qualsiasi monarca abbia mai provato entrando in un nuovo palazzo.

Augustus mi spiegò ora il metodo per fissare l’estremità aperta…

La scatola… e poi, tenendo la candela vicino al tavolo, mi mostrò…

“Un pezzo di corda nera disteso lungo di esso… Questo, disse, proveniva da me.”

Titolo:  
Un nascondiglio tra tutti i meandri necessari, tra il legname…

Un chiodo conficcato nel ponte della stiva, esattamente sotto…

La botola che conduceva nella sua cabina… Con questo cordone avrei potuto…

Posso facilmente trovare la strada per uscire senza il suo aiuto, purché...

Qualsiasi incidente inaspettato potrebbe rendere necessario tale passo. Ora…

Si allontanò, lasciandomi la lanterna insieme ad altro…

“Un’abbondante scorta di ceri e fosforo, e la promessa di pagarmi…”

Visitare il più spesso possibile, senza essere notato.

Avvenne il diciassettesimo giugno.



Rimasi tre giorni e tre notti (quanto più posso giudicare) nel mio...

Titolo:  
Un rifugio… da cui non si esce mai, se non due volte.

Lo scopo di stirare le membra stando eretto tra due casse

Proprio di fronte all’ingresso… Per tutto quel tempo, non ho visto nulla.

Augusto; ma questa situazione non mi causò alcuna preoccupazione, poiché conoscevo bene quella brigata.

Si prevedeva che salpasse da un momento all’altro, e nel caos generale nessuno riusciva a intervenire per fermarlo.

“Facilmente trovano occasioni per avvicinarsi a me… Alla fine, ho sentito…”

“La trappola è aperta e chiusa… Poco dopo, chiamò a bassa voce, chiedendo…”

Tutto andava bene, e se c'era qualcosa che desideravo, “Niente,” risposi.

“Sono quanto più comodo possa essere… Quando partirà la brigata?” “Partirà presto.”

“Devo partire tra meno di mezz’ora,” rispose. “Sono venuto per avvisarti…”

Lo sai. E per [[concepts/fear|paura]], dovresti sentirti a disagio alla mia assenza. Non succederà nulla.

La possibilità di tornare giù per un po’ di tempo, forse per tre o quattro giorni.

Altri pochi giorni… Tutto procede in modo trasparente e regolare. Dopo che me ne sarò andato, tutto verrà chiuso a dovere.

La trappola: ti avvicini furtivamente lungo la corda, fino al punto in cui c’è il chiodo?

“Driven in… Lì troverai il mio orologio: potrebbe esserti utile.”

Non hai luce naturale per misurare il [[motifs/time|tempo]]; immagino che non tu possa sapere quanto tempo sia trascorso.

Sei stato sepolto da soli tre giorni… oggi è il ventesimo. Io…

“Porta l’orologio nella tua scatola… ma temo che venga dimenticato.” Con queste parole…

Salì su.



Dopo circa un’ora dalla sua partenza, percepii chiaramente che la brigata…

Movimento… e mi complimentai con me stesso per aver finalmente iniziato seriamente.

“Un viaggio… Soddisfatto di questa idea, decisi di mettermi d’impegno.”

Facile come possibile… e aspettare che gli eventi seguano il loro corso, fino al momento in cui dovrò agire.

È permesso sostituire la scatola con una più spaziosa, anche se di poco.

“Comode sistemazioni nella cabina… La mia prima preoccupazione è stata quella di sistemarmi al meglio.”

"Guarda... Lasciando la candela accesa, mi mossi a tentoni nel buio, seguendo..."

Titolo:  
Il filo attraverso innumerevoli avvolgimenti… in alcuni dei quali ho scoperto…

Dopo aver percorso una lunga distanza, fui riportato indietro a meno di un piede di distanza.

“O due di una posizione precedente… Alla fine raggiunsi il chiodo…”

Ho ottenuto l’oggetto del mio [[motifs/journey|Viaggio]], e l’ho riportato indietro sano e salvo. Ora.

Esaminò attentamente i libri che erano stati così premurosamente forniti.

La spedizione di Lewis e Clarke alla foce del…

Columbia. Con questo mi sono divertito per un po’ di tempo, finché, crescendo.

“Sonnolento, spensi la luce con grande attenzione e presto mi addormentai…”

Un sonno profondo e tranquillo.



Al risveglio mi sentii stranamente confuso nella mente; trascorse un po’ di tempo.

Prima che riuscissi a ricordare tutte le varie circostanze…

La mia situazione… Gradualmente, però, ricordai tutto. Accendendo una luce…

Ho guardato l’orologio… ma era fermo.

Di conseguenza, non esisteva alcun modo per sapere quanto tempo avessi dormito… Le mie membra…

Ero stretto in uno spazio molto ristretto, e fui costretto a “liberarmi” stando in piedi…

Tra le casse… In quel momento, provando un appetito quasi insaziabile, io…

Mi sono ricordato della carne di montone fredda che avevo appena mangiato…

Prima di andare a [[motifs/sleep|dormire]], trovai qualcosa di davvero eccellente. Che sorpresa per me!

Scoprire che si trova in uno stato di totale decomposizione…

La circostanza in questione mi causò grande preoccupazione, poiché, collegandola…

Il disordine mentale che provai al risveglio… iniziai a pensare…

Deve essermi addormentato per un periodo di [[motifs/time|tempo]] insolitamente lungo.

L’atmosfera tesa e opprimente del ponte di carico potrebbe esserne stata la causa.

E potrebbe, alla fine, portare a risultati estremamente seri. Il mio…

La testa mi faceva un male terribile; avevo l’impressione di respirare con difficoltà…

Difficoltà… In breve, ero sopraffatto da una miriade di preoccupazioni e tristezze.

Sentimenti… Eppure non osavo causare alcun disturbo aprendo…

La trappola… o qualcos’altro; dopo aver caricato l’orologio, mi rassegnai.

Il meglio possibile.



“Per tutte le noiose ventiquattro ore successive, nessuno…”

“Fu un vero sollievo per me… e non potei fare a meno di incolpare Augustus…”

Gravissima negligenza. Quello che mi ha preoccupato di più è stato il fatto che l’[[motifs/water|acqua]] nella mia.

La birra era stata ridotta a circa mezzo pinta, e io soffrivo molto a causa di questo.

Titolo:  
Sete… dopo aver mangiato liberamente quelle salsicce di Bologna… dopo la perdita…

Il mio agnello… Mi sentii molto a disagio e non riuscivo più a provare alcun interesse per nulla.

Nei miei libri. Anch’io ero sopraffatto da un [[motifs/desire|-desiderio]] di [[motifs/sleep|-dormire]], eppure.



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="chapter_01--part_04" data-title="The Narrative of Arthur Gordon Pym of Nantucket — Chapter 2 (part 4)" data-chapter="Chapter 01" data-kind="excerpt" data-tags="archetype/monster,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/dreams,motif/lion,motif/love,motif/memory,motif/rose,motif/sleep,motif/tears,motif/time,motif/water,motif/well,concept/body,concept/despair,concept/mercy,concept/solitude,concept/suffering"></span>


trembled at the thought of indulging it, lest there might exist some

pernicious influence, like that of burning charcoal, in the confined

air of the hold. In the mean [[motifs/time|time]] the roll of the brig told me that we

were far in the main ocean, and a dull humming sound, which reached my

ears as if from an immense distance, convinced me no ordinary gale was

blowing. I could not imagine a reason for the absence of Augustus. We

were surely far enough advanced on our voyage to allow of my going up.

Some accident might have happened to him--but I could think of none

which would account for his [[concepts/suffering|suffering]] me to remain so long a prisoner,

except, indeed, his having suddenly died or fallen overboard, and upon

this idea I could not dwell with any degree of patience. It was

possible that we had been baffled by head winds, and were still in the

near vicinity of Nantucket. This notion, however, I was forced to

abandon; for, such being the case, the brig must have frequently gone

about; and I was entirely satisfied, from her continual inclination to

the larboard, that she had been sailing all along with a steady breeze

on her starboard quarter. Besides, granting that we were still in the

neighbourhood of the island, why should not Augustus have visited me

and informed me of the circumstance? Pondering in this manner upon the

difficulties of my solitary and cheerless condition, I resolved to wait

yet another twenty-four hours, when, if no relief were obtained, I

would make my way to the trap, and endeavour either to hold a parley

with my friend, or get at least a little fresh air through the opening,

and a further supply of [[motifs/water|water]] from his stateroom. While occupied with

this thought, however, I fell, in spite of every exertion to the

contrary, into a state of profound [[motifs/sleep|sleep]], or rather stupor. My [[motifs/dreams|dreams]]

were of the most terrific description. Every species of calamity and

horror befell me. Among other miseries, I was smothered to [[motifs/death|death]]

between huge pillows, by demons of the most ghastly and ferocious

aspect. Immense serpents held me in their embrace, and looked earnestly

in my face with their fearfully shining eyes. Then deserts, limitless,

and of the most forlorn and awe-inspiring character, spread themselves

out before me. Immensely tall trunks of trees, gray and leafless, [[motifs/rose|rose]]

up in endless succession as far as the eye could reach. Their roots

were concealed in wide-spreading morasses, whose dreary [[motifs/water|water]] lay

intensely black, still, and altogether terrible, beneath. And the

strange trees seemed endowed with a human vitality, and, waving to and

fro their skeleton arms, were crying to the silent waters for [[concepts/mercy|mercy]], in

the shrill and piercing accents of the most acute agony and [[concepts/despair|despair]].

The scene changed; and I stood, naked and alone, amid the burning

sand-plains of Zahara. At my feet lay crouched a fierce [[motifs/lion|lion]] of the

tropics. Suddenly his wild eyes opened and fell upon me. With a

convulsive bound he sprang to his feet, and laid bare his horrible

teeth. In another instant there burst from his red throat a roar like

the thunder of the firmament, and I fell impetuously to the earth.

Stifling in a paroxysm of terror, I at last found myself partially

awake. My dream, then, was not all a dream. Now, at least, I was in

possession of my senses. The paws of some huge and real [[archetypes/monster|monster]] were

pressing heavily upon my bosom--his hot breath was in my ear--and his

white and ghastly fangs were gleaming upon me through the gloom.



Had a thousand lives hung upon the movement of a limb or the utterance

of a syllable, I could have neither stirred nor spoken. The beast,

whatever it was, retained his position without attempting any immediate

violence, while I lay in an utterly helpless, and, I fancied, a dying

condition beneath him. I felt that my powers of [[concepts/body|body]] and mind were fast

leaving me--in a word, that I was perishing, and perishing of sheer

fright. My brain swam--I grew deadly sick--my vision failed--even the

glaring eyeballs above me grew dim. Making a last strong effort, I at

length breathed a faint ejaculation to God, and resigned myself to die.

The sound of my voice seemed to arouse all the latent fury of the

animal. He precipitated himself at full length upon my [[concepts/body|body]]; but what

was my astonishment, when, with a long and low whine, he commenced

licking my face and hands with the greatest eagerness, and with the

most extravagant demonstrations of affection and joy! I was bewildered,

utterly lost in amazement--but I could not forget the peculiar whine of

my Newfoundland dog Tiger, and the odd manner of his caresses I [[motifs/well|well]]

knew. It was he. I experienced a sudden rush of [[motifs/blood|blood]] to my temples--a

giddy and overpowering sense of deliverance and reanimation. I [[motifs/rose|rose]]

hurriedly from the mattress upon which I had been lying, and, throwing

myself upon the neck of my faithful follower and friend, relieved the

long oppression of my bosom in a flood of the most passionate [[motifs/tears|tears]].



As upon a former occasion, my conceptions were in a state of the

greatest indistinctness and confusion after leaving the mattress. For a

long [[motifs/time|time]] I found it nearly impossible to connect any ideas--but, by

very slow degrees, my thinking faculties returned, and I again called

to [[motifs/memory|memory]] the several incidents of my condition. For the presence of

Tiger I tried in vain to account; and after busying myself with a

thousand different conjectures respecting him, was forced to content

myself with rejoicing that he was with me to share my dreary [[concepts/solitude|solitude]],

and render me comfort by his caresses. Most people [[motifs/love|love]] their dogs--but

for Tiger I had an affection far more ardent than common; and never,

certainly, did any creature more truly deserve it. For seven years he


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

Tremava al solo pensiero di concederselo, temendo che potesse esserci qualche conseguenza negativa…

Influenza dannosa, simile a quella del carbone ardente, in ambienti chiusi

L’aria nel ponte di carico. Nel frattempo, il dondolio della nave mi fece capire che stavamo.

“Erano lontani, nel cuore dell’oceano, e si udiva un suono sommesso e continuo che raggiungeva le mie orecchie…”

“Orecchie che sembravano provenire da una distanza immensa… mi convinsero che non si trattava di una tempesta ordinaria.”

Assenza di Augustus

Dovevamo sicuramente essere abbastanza avanti nel nostro viaggio per permettermi di salire.

Qualche incidente potrebbe essere accaduto a lui… ma non mi viene in mente nulla.

Questo spiegherebbe il suo [[concepts/suffering|soffrire]] nel lasciarmi prigioniero per così tanto tempo.

Tranne, ovviamente, il caso in cui morisse improvvisamente o cadesse in mare…

Non riuscivo proprio a sopportare quell’idea, per niente al mondo.

“È possibile che siamo stati ostacolati da venti contrari, e che ancora ci troviamo nel…”

Nelle immediate vicinanze di Nantucket… Tuttavia, questa idea mi costrinse a…

Abbandonare… poiché, in tal caso, la brigata deve essersi spostata frequentemente.

Titolo:  
E fui completamente soddisfatto, data la sua costante propensione…

A dritta, verso dove aveva navigato per tutto il tempo, con una brezza costante.

Sul suo lato di dritta… Inoltre, ammesso che fossimo ancora nel…

Il quartiere dell’isola… Perché mai Augusto non sarebbe dovuto venire a trovarmi?

E mi ha informato su quella situazione? Riflettendo in questo modo…

"#Le difficoltà della mia condizione solitaria e triste mi spinsero a decidere di aspettare."

Ancora altre ventiquattro ore… In caso non si ottenesse alcun sollievo, io…

Mi avvicinerei alla trappola e cercherei di negoziare…

“Con il mio amico… o almeno prendere un po’ d’aria fresca attraverso quell’apertura…”

E un ulteriore rifornimento di [[motifs/water|acqua]] dal suo cabina. Mentre era impegnato in.

Questo pensiero, tuttavia… nonostante tutti i miei sforzi…

Al contrario, in uno stato di profondo [[motifs/sleep|sonno]], o meglio, di stordimento. I miei [[motifs/dreams|sogni]].

Titolo:  
Di una descrizione davvero terribile… Ogni tipo di calamità…

Il terrore mi colpì. Tra altre sofferenze, fui soffocato fino alla [[motifs/death|morte]].
